### Settled

**/start outcome home.** SPEC-2 Edit (a) §7.2 `starting → failed` line is the one home: "POST /v1/sessions/{id}/start fails after its ready → starting write; or STARTING_TIMEOUT expired"; no error codes and no "already reclaimed the prepared pod" qualifier (that stays only as D8's reason).
**Why the qualifier went.** Pre-bind /start returns (ResolvePool, PoolWarmingError, credential resolution, isolation refusal in pod_launch.go) would get no write and strand the row in `starting`; CODE-6 item 4 gives them the by-name reclaim.
**Edits (b), (g), (f).** Edits (b) and (g) point at the §7.2 `starting` edges; Edit (f) retargets the §15.1 STARTING_FAILED catalog row to "Retryable by creating a new session, because the failed start leaves this session `failed`".
**STARTING_FAILED stays TRANSIENT.** 503 with Retry-After; this keeps §4.7.1 slot-bind refusal, §5.2 pin read, the §6.2 "retryable STARTING_FAILED fallback" clause, the SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED row, and docs error-catalog.md:128 true unedited. Recategorizing PERMANENT was rejected.
**starting → resume_pending deleted.** No writer exists (failure.go applyFailure no-ops on `starting`; /start is synchronous); Edit (h) drops `starting` from §7.3 **Retry accounting.**
**Edge sites.** Only spec/07:180, :226, :456 and spec/06:302 named `starting → resume_pending`; Edits (a), (b), (h), (g) stage all four. No docs diagram carries the edge.
**Setup inside `starting`.** Edit (i), one sentence in the §6.2 `maxSessionAge` timer table `starting` row: a concurrent-workspace slot's setup at /start runs inside `starting`, and a setup outlasting it fails with STARTING_TIMEOUT; reason in D8.
**Edit (a) cites §6.2.** The `starting → failed` line cites `see §6.2` instead of `see §6.3 maxStartingTimeoutSeconds`; the term appears only in §6.2.
**Edit (k).** §7.1 **Gateway-owned terminal writes:** gains the `starting → failed` write a failed /start makes and drops "two"/"those two"; the quoted text matches spec/07:124.
**failStarting.** CODE-6 item 3 adds hook-path writer `failStarting` in session_terminal.go (context.WithoutCancel, BeginTerminal, writeTerminal guard refusing unless the locked row is `starting` and setting FailureReason, then afterFailed), sibling of `failFinalizing`.
**CODE-6 item 4.** `starting` joins isPreRunningClaimState and `revokedPreRunning` (users_invalidate.go); tier-7a `startHeldBeforeCommit` expects `starting`.
**CODE-6 item 6.** ValidTransitions and sessionStateMachineEdges point at SPEC-2 Edits (a), (c), and (e) without listing edges; ValidTransitions also carries the Edit (c) any-state edges.
**CODE-6 item 7 (SDK Start one-shot).** Each client SDK's Start sends once via a private per-call request-loop parameter; no public option and no code-keyed exemption. DOC-1 covers the three sdk-examples **Retries** paragraphs; TEST-1 item 9 adds one request-count test per SDK.
**TEST-1 item 9 assertion.** Asserts no claim, slot, binding, or lease is left held, rather than "released once", because afterFailed repeats a by-name reclaim the binder already did.
**TEST-1 item 13.** Its second half (tier 4) is the STARTING_TIMEOUT-mid-/start race; FailureReason staying STARTING_TIMEOUT discriminates against failStarting overwriting it.
**CODE-6 placement.** startHeldBeforeCommit, ValidTransitions, and the failStarting inventory entry live in CODE-6 so TEST-1 is not renumbered.
**CODE-6 exclusions stand.** create_and_start.go and delegated_child_materialize.go hold no `ready` row across a launch; summary Defects gained "The delegated child commit reuses the `/start` transition helper."
**Edit (c) any-state paragraph.** Force-terminate → failed (FORCE_TERMINATED); budget exhaustion, LLM fallback exhaustion, credential lease, orphan cleanup, and perChildMaxAge → expired. `maxSessionAge` is not in it.
**maxSessionAge per-state edges.** `starting → expired` (Edit (a)), `resume_pending → expired` and `resuming → expired` (Edit (e), retitled), a §6.2 `resuming → expired` bullet (Edit (d), "two replacements and one insertion"); `awaiting_client_action → expired` already exists. D12 holds the reason.
**Legality basis citations.** D12, CODE-3's doc-comment bullet, and REC-1 item 1 cite the §7.2 per-state `→ expired` edges plus the existing `awaiting_client_action → expired` edge.
**Edit (j) re-scope.** §7.2 **Mid-resume terminal transitions — snapshot-close semantics.** and **Pre-attach terminal collapse.** are re-scoped by source state ("A terminal transition that finds the session in `resuming`"/`resume_pending`), mirroring §7.3 **Exits from `resuming`.**; Edit (d) also replaces the §6.2 `resuming` "(b) ... cancelled / completed via snapshot-close" summary.
**Edit (j) replacement (b).** Points at **Pre-attach terminal collapse.** for `resume_pending` authority; the "The `resuming` failure table in §6.2 enumerates only…" sentence is kept.
**Code matches Edit (j).** MarkTerminated bumps coordination_generation on every terminal write from `resuming`; sealWorkspace skips the Sealer with no binding; no code deliverable (terminal_mark.go:32-51, seal.go:107-110).
**SPEC-4 second sentence deleted.** The §10.1.1 list assigning resume driver, idle suspension, suspended-pod release, and backlog resume to the coordinating replica is gone.
**Backlog resume runs on the leader.** Podless suspended backlog recheck is leader-gated (leaderGate "suspended-backlog-recheck", leader_sweeps.go:476) plus once on the releasing replica; ResumeBacklog acts only for pod-holding rows on the coordinator.
**Coordinator-gated hooks.** Only DriveResume, SuspendIdle, ReleaseSuspendedPod, and ResumeBacklog gate on `coordinatesSession`; summary D2 lists those four, and the Non-goals "Leader-gating the whole watchdog" bullet points at D2.
**Expiry warning on every replica.** OnSessionExpiringSoon has no gate; summary Defects "**Duplicate `session_expiring_soon` emissions across replicas.**" and the F-10.1.36 out-of-scope line carry it.
**Migration 0192.** `ADD COLUMN state_entered_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`, no backfill, no separate SET NOT NULL (§10.5 DEFAULT remedy, lint Pass 4; existing rows restart windows once, 0182 precedent).
**Trigger sessions_stamp_state_entry.** Permanent BEFORE UPDATE trigger: when `NEW.state IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.state AND NEW.state_entered_at IS NOT DISTINCT FROM OLD.state_entered_at`, set it to NEW.updated_at; covers old-replica updateSQL writes in the rolling window.
**CODE-7 item 2.** The request_input exit write runs on context.WithoutCancel(ctx) bounded by sessionstore.DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout, with no retry (RecordPodRelease precedent).
**D13 persisted input wait (owner decision, 2026-10-27).** The row carries `InputWaitID`, a gateway wait ID drawn from `idFn` at registration (CODE-7 item 1); W1 stamps it even on a row already `input_required`; W2 applies only when the locked row names its own wait; the column goes in migration 0192. R1 runs on the replica that accepts a pod process's `LNK-GWCONTROL` connection (one per pod process to one replica, §28.3) and restores or re-points that pod's `input_required` rows whose named wait it does not hold, with W4 re-arming after a failed W2 and C1 draining at shutdown. Rejected: accept-and-disclose, a time-bounded restore, an exit-write retrier, a non-waiter replica restore, and the Sweeper "Stale wait" restore (the lease holder is not the waiter); do not reintroduce any of them. SPEC-2 stages no wait text (see Traps, Replaced: SPEC-2 Edit (l)).
**CODE-7 item 4 "State gates".** Renamed from "Running-only gates"; table includes isActiveForFreshness and mapStateToResponsesStatus.
**TEST-1 renumbering.** G4's tier-4 test is item 14 (old 14-22 became 15-23); old item 21 is item 22, a pointer to the CODE-7 item 4 table; item 19 is the off-pass blocking-hook test, item 18 the List/Update fault-injection test.
**CODE-4 item 1.** ErrNotFound is treated as superseded in every sweep; only other non-sentinel Update errors join Tick's `errors.Join` (expireSuspendedIfDue precedent).
**Edge-cases bullet deleted.** "its hook runs once, on the pass that saw the nil `Update`" is gone; §12.3 lost-ack row owns the outcome.
**Summary Watch-out bullets deleted.** StampStateEntry, defer end(), w.clock() read, and terminal_seams_wiring_test count bullets are stated at CODE-1, CODE-4 items 3-4, CODE-8 item 1.
**Checklist reconciled.** S2 reads "SPEC-2. Lands Edits (a) to (l) in §6.2, §7.1, §7.2, §7.3, §8.8, §15.1, and §29.3 in one commit"; DOC-1 is S13 (docs, after S2, S10, and S11); TEST-1 is whole in S14; REC-1 is S15; S8 (CODE-5) depends on S2.
**REC-1 item 4.** Lists the §7.2 `/start` edges and the per-state `maxSessionAge` expiry edges.
**No human decisions.** OD-1 resolved to option A (gateway `idFn` wait ID), and no OPEN routes a decision to the human; summary **Open decisions for human to make** says so.
**Plan conformance.** P3 sites: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3109-3124, :1241, :2717, :3347-3349; every deliverable staged or declined (D2, D13, D7, Non-goals, REC-1 item 6).
**Recorded divergences.** "Owner is the watchdog, leader-gated" (D2, Non-goals) and the R20 overlap (D13) are recorded; do not refile.
**Round-4 spec lenses.** Client-surface, docs, performance, and security filed nothing in spec round 4.
**/start return codes.** /start returns many codes (SLOT_FAILED, RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE, WARM_POOL_EXHAUSTED, CREDENTIAL_POOL_EXHAUSTED, USER_CREDENTIAL_NOT_FOUND, TOKEN_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED, 422 isolation codes) via writePodClaimError.
**Shipped /start.** Today a launch failure leaves the row `ready` so the client retries /start (start.go:83-89); STARTING_FAILED is emitted only by two-step /start.
**Launch bound.** §5.2 bounds the /start launch context by maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds (600 s); §5.2 Slot assignment atomicity counts it into claimOrphanTimeout.
**finalizing ≥ setup rule.** Checked only against declared `setupPolicy.timeoutSeconds` in validateSetupPolicy; undeclared gets DefaultSetupPolicyTimeoutSeconds 300 at bind, unchecked.
**§6.2 timer table.** Headed "**`maxSessionAge` timer behavior across states:**", one physical line per row.
**maxAgeExpirable.** `!IsTerminal(st) && st != StateSuspended`; ActiveAge accrues only in starting, running, input_required, so created/ready/finalizing never expire; per-state edges cover every reachable state.
**Paused means evaluated.** §6.2:264 evaluation is state-general; a paused row expires only if it left a counting state already over cap.
**resuming over cap.** Reachable only when the cap drops mid-resume; readResumable refuses an over-cap resume_pending row.
**starting → expired.** `starting` is entered only from `ready`, so it fires only when the cap is below maxStartingTimeoutSeconds ("secondary bound").
**resuming is internal.** §15.1:672 keeps it off the wire; the §7.2 "In both cases ... {cancelled|completed}" sentence and spec/04 §4.2 "mid-resume terminal collapses" parenthetical need no widening.
**FORCE_TERMINATED.** Absent from spec/ before Edit (c); code writes it with FailureClassRuntime (session_admin.go:83-84); §24.11 says only "transitions immediately to failed".
**Orphan and fallback terminals.** Orphan cleanup writes `expired` (§8.10 says "terminated"); spec names no terminal state for CREDENTIAL_FALLBACK_EXHAUSTED; BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED → failed is a different trigger.
**Cross-replica fencing.** §7.3 **Exits from `resuming`.** fences terminal writes on `resuming`; a `resume_pending → expired` is safe because the driver's CAS entry write fails and releases the pod (§7.3 **Resume driver.**).
**Anchors.** All quoted SPEC-1..4 anchors resolve uniquely as of round 4; the Edit (j) step-5/"Terminal handling otherwise identical." phrase occurs twice ("each occurrence"); the old Pre-attach label has no inbound reference.
**Design bullet.** spec-changes `/start` Design bullet enumerates "(a), (b), and (f) to (i)".
**No observability surface.** No metric, alert, Helm value, or schema identifier is added; docs never name maxStartingTimeoutSeconds or STARTING_TIMEOUT.
**No new writes.** state_entered_at rides state-change Updates; SPEC-4 recheck locks only due rows; last_agent_activity_at is persisted coalesced 1/s and its restart-on-entry is stated only at spec/06:266.
**SuspendIdle guard.** Gates on the coordination lease and guards on LastAgentActivityAt and binding generation, so "every replica runs the other sweeps" lets no non-coordinator suspend.
**Trusted sources.** state_entered_at is store-stamped, orphan anchor is root TerminatedAt, perChildMaxAge comes from the gateway-written child lease plus store CreatedAt; a root row carries no DelegationLease.
**Locked-row recheck home.** SPEC-4's §10.1.1 sentence is the only spec home; sweepResuming's CoordinationGeneration guard is equivalent.
**Claim-register rule.** The plan's "claim-register row" rule applies only to §28 statements.
**ValidTransitions.** No runtime caller; holds {Ready, Starting} and {Starting, ResumePending}; state_test.go sessionStateMachineEdges is hardcoded, lacks {Ready, Starting}, holds {Starting, ResumePending}; illegal list does not collide with Edit (c).
**pgstore writes.** The only `UPDATE sessions` is pgstore updateSQL; Update stamps via pgtenant.MonotonicNext, so a Go state stamp always differs and the trigger never overrides it.
**Create paths.** memstore createLocked and pgstore create back Create, CreateIfGeneration, CreateReserved, CreateWithTranscript and default CreatedAt there.
**Stamp callers.** AccrueSessionAge has three callers (memstore, pgstore, SteppedClockStore.Update); StampStateEntry needs the same; steppedstore `translate` must carry StateEnteredAt (CODE-1 target).
**windowLeft.** resume_driver.go `windowLeft` is the only UpdatedAt-anchored deadline outside the watchdog; added to CODE-2.
**Idempotency on 5xx.** Middleware releases the key on any 5xx, so a retried /start re-executes against the failed row.
**§15.6.** Says only "error handling with retries"; the Start one-shot needs no spec edit.
**SDK loops.** Go `do`, Python `_do`, TS `do` retry any retryable response and transport error; Start routes through `transition`/`_transition`, which must forward the private parameter; Python AsyncClient.start delegates via `_run`.
**SDK tests.** sdks/client/go/lenny (*_test.go), sdks/client/python/tests, sdks/client/typescript/test (*.test.mjs).
**failStarting inventory.** Routes through writeTerminal, so only `writeTerminalCallers` gains it.
**Revoke reclaim.** ReclaimRevokedPreRunning is terminalReclaimPreRunning, which skips a session bound on this replica and runs before the step-2 binding release.
**Open Responses drift.** mapStateToResponsesStatus returns `queued` for created/finalizing/ready/suspended/resume_pending/awaiting_client_action/expired; docs/api/open-responses.md disagrees; filed in summary Defects and REC-1 item 5.
**defer end() list.** expireIdle (watchdog.go:1249), expireSuspendedIfDue (suspended.go:91), expireOrphan (orphancleanup.go:239), failOrphan (orphansession.go:360); CODE-4 item 3 is the home.
**failOrphan clock.** Reads `now := r.clock()` before Update; CODE-4 item 4 does not describe it; harmless once its hook is off-pass.
**Reconciler wiring.** startReconcilerWorkers runs before startLeaderElectedSweeps, so CODE-8's registration sees a non-nil reconciler.
**Fallback mirror lag.** §4.6.1 pod-claim fallback reads MirrorLagSeconds from the store directly, so leader-gating the reconciler (CODE-8) does not starve it.
**MCP status.** get_session_status renders row.State verbatim through GET /v1/sessions/{id}; DOC-1 stages `input_required` in the docs/api/mcp.md status values and a Task lifecycle mapping row ("Task requires input", the §8.8 MCP Tasks `input_required`). docs/api/open-responses.md has no spec-backed mapping and stays a summary Defects bullet.
**Terminate preconditions.** After 0105 the precondition table admits `input_required` for /terminate (SPEC-2 Edit (l) adds the §7.2 `input_required → completed` edge and lists the sub-state in the §15.1 terminate row) and refuses it for /interrupt with 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION under the §15.1 running-only interrupt row (CODE-7 item 4, D13). CODE-7 item 4 also rewrites both entries' code comments.
**Old-anchor tests.** resume_sweeps_guard_test.go "window restarted by a later write" and orphancleanup_test.go zero-TerminatedAt roots break under CODE-2 and CODE-5.
**transitionStart.** Shared by start.go:164 and delegated_child_materialize.go:181; its body sets `running`.
**Tenant guard.** Any whole-table `UPDATE sessions` in a migration needs `SET LOCAL lenny.allow_all_sentinel='true'` and `app.current_tenant='__all__'` (0181 precedent).
**Rollout order.** The migrate Job runs before the gateway rolls, so old binaries write against the new schema for the whole window.
**Lost-ack row.** §12.3: a committed terminal write the store reports as an error runs no pipeline; orphan-claim detection backstops it; pgstore re-reads last_write_id so only unreadable commits reach the sweep.

### Traps

**MISTAKE: maxSessionAge in Edit (c).** Round 1 put the age expiry in Edit (c)'s any-state paragraph. "Every non-terminal state" includes `suspended` (§6.2 trigger paragraph spec/06:293 and podless paragraph :235 exclude it), and `ready`/`finalizing` (timer does not run). Do not re-add it in any form, scoped or by reference ("from each state §6.2 evaluates it in" is ambiguous because the table says "Paused" for both suspended and resume_pending), and add no `suspended → expired` age line. Point reviewers at D12. perChildMaxAge does stay any-state.
**MISTAKE: Edge-cases "hook runs once".** The bullet claimed a committed-but-errored sweep write runs its hook once on the pass that saw nil Update; no pass sees nil, so it runs zero times. Do not re-add it, and do not add a hook run on the next-pass superseded path: the real writer may have dispatched it, and §12.3 accepts zero runs.
**No /start code lists.** Do not add an error-code enumeration (STARTING_FAILED, SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED, SLOT_FAILED) to the §7.2 edge, Edits (b)/(g), CODE-6 item 3, or TEST-1 item 9; each list was a finding. CODE-6 item 3 stamps "the code of the error it returns". (USEFUL, spec.2 performance)
**Leave §4.7.1 and §5.2 alone.** spec/04 §4.7.1 slot-bind refusal and spec/05 pin-read sentence name the code only; the §7.2 edge carries the outcome. Do not re-file them (USEFUL: the TRANSIENT decision settles them).
**Withdrawn: starting ≥ setup rejection.** A `maxStartingTimeoutSeconds >= setupTimeoutSeconds` admission rule mirroring finalizing would reject every concurrent-workspace pool under 120 s / 300 s defaults, or loosen the watchdog for all pools, and needs a pool × runtime × flag check. Per-session starting+setup deadlines and re-stamping `starting` were also rejected. Stands while Edit (i) stands.
**Withdrawn: STARTING_FAILED PERMANENT / retryable:false.** Rejected twice: it falsifies §4.7.1, §5.2, §6.2 Client visibility, and the SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED row, and leaves the other retryable /start codes (RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE, WARM_POOL_EXHAUSTED, CREDENTIAL_POOL_EXHAUSTED, SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE) broken. A STARTING_FAILED-keyed SDK exemption and Idempotency-Key replay were rejected too. Stands while CODE-6 item 7 stands.
**Withdrawn: starting → ready rollback.** Keeping the reclaimed-pod qualifier and rolling back to `ready` is rejected in Non-goals.
**No close-sequence pointer in Edit (c).** Do not append a pointer or carve-out to Edit (c) or the "`resume_pending → completed` edge is the terminal path" sentence; Edit (j)'s source-state re-scope covers them. The spec/07:236 collapse sentence and spec/04:167 parenthetical stay untouched.
**No §10.1.1 replica list.** Do not re-add a sentence assigning backlog resume, the §6.2 graceful pod release, or the resume driver to a replica; §7.2 **Backlog resume.** runs the podless recheck on the leader.
**§8.10 orphan cleanup is not §10.1.4.** SPEC-4 "orphan cleanup" is the §8.10 delegation-tree job (no leader gating stated); the §10.1.4 orphan session reconciler is leader-only. Do not file SPEC-4 as contradicting it (USEFUL).
**§6.2 has no edge block.** The only code block with starting/resume_pending/resuming edge lines is §7.2; do not search §6.2 for copies (USEFUL).
**Edit (a) Replace block.** spec-changes.md:53 quotes `see §6.3 maxStartingTimeoutSeconds` verbatim as the match anchor; do not edit it.
**§7.1 starting_timeout row.** spec/07:107 carries the same false §6.3 link; it predates 0105 and is not a finding against it (tracked under Deferred).
**Per-state edges vs Edit (c).** Edit (c) states perChildMaxAge and lease/budget expiry from every non-terminal state beside narrower existing code-block lines (spec/07:190, :203, :221). Judged subsumed; if drift appears, reduce rather than re-synchronise.
**Withdrawn: SET NOT NULL / backfill / nullable.** Do not re-add a separate `ALTER COLUMN state_entered_at SET NOT NULL`: lint Pass 4 inspects only ADD COLUMN, so it passes while breaking §10.5. A backfill needs the 0181 SET LOCAL pair and rewrites the table under live old replicas; nullable makes NULL read as infinitely old. Stands while migration 0192's DEFAULT stands.
**Trigger lifetime and down order.** Do not schedule a Phase 3 trigger removal. Down migration drops trigger, then function, then column (0108 precedent); dropping only the column makes every UPDATE fail on NEW.state_entered_at.
**UTC timestamps.** spec/12 **UTC timestamps.** says `now() AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`, but migrations use `DEFAULT now()` 82 times and spec/12 itself writes `DEFAULT now()`; 0192 follows the tree. Not staged.
**writePodClaimError writes the response.** It derives the code inside its switch and returns nothing, so failStarting's FailureReason needs a split of mapping from writing; stamping a fallback STARTING_FAILED on every exit fails TEST-1 item 9.
**Reclaim after binder release.** With `starting` pre-running, a starting → failed after the binder reclaimed calls ReclaimClaimed on a deleted claim; terminalReclaimPreRunning logs and continues, as failFinalizing accepts.
**failStarting inventory.** Put it in `writeTerminalCallers` only; a terminalWriterInventory or TerminalContextWriterKeys row makes TestTerminalWriterInventoryMatchesTheCode fail.
**Stale doc comment.** usage.go isPreRunningClaimState says it "deliberately excludes `starting`" (citing proposal 0007 §4.6, not spec); CODE-6 item 4 makes it false and the implementor rewrites it.
**Tier-7a fixture.** full_revoke_sweep_terminal_race_test.go:110-112 asserts a held /start leaves `ready`; CODE-6 makes it `starting`.
**No status_change on ready → starting.** No generic store hook publishes status_change; commitStart emits at start.go:171, but CODE-6 item 1's new write has none staged (lifecycle.go:214-226).
**transitionStart callers.** CODE-6 item 5 ("no longer short-circuits") must leave the delegated child `created → running` commit landing on `running` (USEFUL; the proposal text still does not state the body).
**Withdrawn: SDK retry alternatives.** A public no-retry option or a field on Python/TS RequestOptions is rejected; the parameter is private and only Start sets it. The Go SDK had the same Targets omission as Python and TS and was fixed.
**SDK test discriminator.** The per-SDK Start test must assert a request count of one; a stub always answering 503 STARTING_FAILED yields STARTING_FAILED from a retrying SDK too.
**DOC-1 sentence placement.** Each sdk-examples **Retries** paragraph ends "Override the policy with …:" before its code block; the Start sentence goes before that sentence, not after the colon.
**DOC-1 scope.** docs/client-guide/error-handling.md:35, :142 and spec/04 §4.7.1 stay untouched by design; do not widen DOC-1 without a finding.
**Switch-based gates.** isActiveForFreshness (freshness.go:209) and mapStateToResponsesStatus (open_responses.go:569) leave input_required out and escape a `== session.StateRunning` grep.
**Expiry warning wording.** Do not re-add the expiry warning to the Non-goal or D2 as a coordinating-replica action.
**Plan-conformance trap.** BUILD-GAPS F-7.2.43 (created-state expiry writes failed where §15.1 says expired) names P3 owner (BUILD-GAPS.md:7618), but the plan's P3 list omits it; the summary now carries it in Non-goals and the Defects "stay open" bullet, and REC-1 item 6 records that 0105 does not stage it.
**Not a security regression.** CODE-6 item 4's `starting` in isPreRunningClaimState matches shipped `ready` behavior: the by-name per-slot reclaim sends no Shutdown and counts no leak although a concurrent-workspace slot may be mid-setup. Filed as the summary Defects bullet "A by-name reclaim of a pre-running concurrent-workspace slot counts no leak."
**Not findings.** Unbounded CODE-4 hook goroutines (non-goals reject a pool; budgetSessionTerminator precedent), /start write rates, the 600 s launch vs 120 s watchdog, and coordinator loss mid-/start waiting out STARTING_TIMEOUT (no adoption of `starting`, §10.1.1) were judged not findings.
**Docs edges left out.** DOC-1 stages `starting → failed` (state-machines.md diagram and table, session-lifecycle.md diagram), and `ready → starting` is already drawn (state-machines.md, session-lifecycle.md, concepts.md). The `starting → expired`, `resume_pending → expired`, and `resuming → expired` edges and the Edit (c) any-state writes stay out of the docs diagrams, which were never exhaustive, so the pages are incomplete rather than false. Do not refile them.
**R1 failure and load.** A3 gives a failed R1 run's outcome (row unchanged, R1 again before the connection's next call carrying a session ID), so the call itself is dispatched; the W4 replica-wide re-arm burst was judged within budget by the performance lens and D13 settles W4. Do not refile.
**perChildMaxAge docs.** docs/runtime-author-guide/delegation.md and platform-tools.md ("Max wall-clock seconds for the child") stay true after SPEC-3.
**Replaced: request-ID wait identity.** The row once carried the caller-supplied `requestId` of the newest wait. It lost because a runtime can reuse a `requestId` on its next connection to another replica, so the old wait's exit finds its own ID and writes `running` under a live wait (F-11.3.39). CODE-7 item 1 stamps a gateway `idFn` wait ID; OD-1 resolved to option A.
**Replaced: accepted stale row and Sweeper restore.** Old CODE-7 rows 8 to 11 accepted a stale `input_required` row after a failed exit write or waiter exit, with no recovery owner. They lost to D13's connection-boundary recovery (CODE-7 item 2 R1, rows X4, K1, A2, and A3). Do not re-add a coordination Sweeper restore: the lease holder is not the waiter.
**Replaced: SPEC-2 Edit (l).** The §7.2 stale-row note and the §6.2 idle-row "or it leaves `input_required`" replacement are deleted, and SPEC-2 stages no wait text. The replacement dropped the existing `maxSessionAge` bound and added no rule; a forwarded call whose `LNK-GWCONTROL` connection ended is a cancelled request on the existing §7.2 edge (D13).
**Pruned: CODE-7 item 3 outcome table (orchestrator.prune-code7).** W0 to W4, R1, and C1 are normative; the table keeps only rows E0, E4, X4, X5, K1, N1, N2, A2, A3, and P1, which the rules leave implicit, with columns Row, Case, Session row afterwards, and Recovery owner. Do not re-add rows that restate an applied or superseded branch (old E1 to E3, X1 to X3, A1, A4), a Published or `InputWaits` column, or a claim that the table is complete. TEST-1 items 7 and 8 test each rule directly plus one case per kept row; do not restore a row-by-row mirror.
**Edge `/mcp` serves request_input.** The edge endpoint and the platform tool bridge share one `mcp.Server`. CODE-7 W0 persists only calls carrying an `inputwaitrecovery` connection record; whether the edge serves the tool at all is a summary Defects bullet, not a staged change.

- OPEN: §6.2 **Graceful pod release during extended suspension** names no replica; code gates on coordinator; not filed [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1].
- OPEN: SPEC-4 "every replica runs the other sweeps" beside §6.2 Idle suspension coordinator wording; judged reconcilable [spec.2.review-mechanism.1].
- OPEN: SPEC-4 names an undefined "session watchdog" and the awaiting_client_action sweep is anchored in §7.3; not filed [spec.4.review-mechanism.1].
- UNVERIFIED: whether tests/spec-map.json carries a §10.5 key; S13 implementor checks [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1].
- UNVERIFIED: whether the `suspendedSince` zero-SuspendedAt fallback is reachable for any post-0092 row [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1].

### Deferred

None. The orchestrator.pre-approval-touchup pass retired the four entries reconcile.1 had re-filed: the docs session-edge omissions and the perChildMaxAge docs rows (see Settled "Docs edges left out." and "perChildMaxAge docs."), the SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED row (reconcile.3 closed it under "STARTING_FAILED stays TRANSIENT."), and the §7.1 `starting_timeout` link defect (a summary Defects bullet).

FACT: The `lenny/request_input` waiter lives on the coordinator. spec/29 states an `inReplyTo` to an `input_required` session is "Forward[ed] to the coordinator, which resolves the outstanding `lenny/request_input` call". So the CODE-7 item 3 check "this replica has no pending wait" on the renewing or taking-over replica is the right locality. In steady state it writes nothing, because the coordinator holds the wait. FACT: Write-rate check on the round-2 hunks. The 0192 trigger is one plpgsql call per `UPDATE sessions`, and `sessions` already carries `lenny_tenant_guard`. `ADD COLUMN ... DEFAULT now()` is metadata-only, because `now()` is STABLE. The item 3 revert is at most one write per stale row, after which the row is `running`. A takeover already makes a synchronous `RecordHandoff` write per row, so item 3 adds at most one more per taken-over `input_required` row. None of these reaches the bar. Item 2's write already logs its errors, and item 3 should log them the same way, as `upsertMirror` does. This was not filed: the proposal does not direct returning the error, so it stays below the bar.

### [non-spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 1 hoists `acquireCoordinationLease` ahead of the `ready → starting` write (both before `launchOnPod`); a refused acquire answers STARTING_FAILED and writes nothing; item 3's failure exits call `releaseCoordinationLease` on WithoutCancel after `failStarting` whatever it returned; TEST-1 item 9 pins held lease during launch, foreign holder, and the 409 keeping the first start's lease — BECAUSE §10.1.1 **Primary:** and the §29.3 terminate/DELETE rows name `starting` as a leased state, and the early-commit paths already acquire before their commit (create_and_start.go:494, delegated_child_materialize.go:158, resume_driver.go:146) — ALTERNATIVES: write-then-acquire (a `starting` row with no lease for one Redis round trip; reverse of every precedent); spec edit saying `starting` holds no lease (weakens spec); TTL lapse after a failed /start (falsifies item 9's "no lease held").
DECISION: item 1 also releases the lease on a non-409 error of the `starting` write (one clause, plus one TEST-1 item 9 assertion). The design covered only the 409 case — BECAUSE the Sweeper renews a lease whose prior holder is this replica on any non-terminal row, `ready` included, so a lease stranded on a `ready` row is never released while this replica lives, and every /start served by another replica then fails STARTING_FAILED on ErrHeld — ALTERNATIVES: no release (strand); release on 409 too (drops a concurrent same-replica /start's lease, the design's doNotDo).
FACT: Sweeper.sweepRow renews whenever priorHolder == this replica, bound or not, in any non-terminal non-podless-suspended state; terminal rows go to sweepTerminal with no Acquire, so a lease left on a terminal row lapses on TTL — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:428-434, :479-493
FACT: handleStart validates the `ready` precondition on the entry read before any launch, so a sequential /start on a `starting` row gets 409 before the acquire; only a racing /start on another replica reaches ErrHeld (STARTING_FAILED) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:49-63
WATCHOUT: between the acquire and the `starting` write the row is `ready` with a held lease, which §10.1.1 **Primary:** ("A session in `created`, `finalizing`, or `ready` ... holds no lease") does not describe; judged a sub-request window inherent to acquire-first, matching create_and_start.go, which acquires before the row exists. Do not re-file it as a spec contradiction without weighing the write-first alternative's unleased `starting` window — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:28
FACT: §29.3 /start row (`ready`: no holder, serving replica performs the start) and §29.2 item 11 (`unstated` initial acquisition) stay true after CODE-6; the related lookup flagged both — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:585, :209-215

### [non-spec.4.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 1 entry write sits after `InputWaits.Register` and before the `elicitation_request` publish; a non-ErrSuperseded error calls `InputWaits.Cancel` and returns the error raw, publishing nothing; TEST-1 item 7 asserts it with empty `PendingForSession` as the discriminating assertion — BECAUSE the registry has no TTL (only Resolve/Cancel remove an entry), so a leaked registration blocks item 2 and item 3 for the session on that replica, and waiting on a `running` row reopens F-11.3.39 — ALTERNATIVES: retry (still needs a final-failure outcome), item 3 mirror repair of `running` rows with a pending wait (second mechanism, exposed until a pass), write before Register (item 3 can revert in the gap; ErrDuplicate strands the row).
FACT: the shipped handler publishes `elicitation_request` immediately after Register and returns Register's error raw (`return mcp.ToolResult{}, err`) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:1024-1050; inputwait.go:114 Register, :170 Cancel (idempotent, closes channel).
WATCHOUT: Register increments `consumed[sessionID]` and Cancel never decrements it, so a failed entry write consumes a `one_shot` runtime's single request_input round (the existing timeout/cancel paths behave the same). Not staged; raise only if a lens files it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/inputwait/inputwait.go:123, :127-131.

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 1 acquires REG-COORDLEASE (`acquireCoordinationLease`) BEFORE the `ready → starting` write, not after it — BECAUSE every existing pre-commit acquire (single-call /start, delegated-child materialize, resume driver `attemptResume`) acquires ahead of its state write, and acquire-first makes §10.1.1 **Primary:** and the §29.3 terminate/DELETE `starting` rows hold with no window — ALTERNATIVES: write-then-acquire (a `starting` row without a lease for one Redis RTT; an off-holder terminate there fails closed); a spec edit saying `starting` holds no lease until bind (weakens the spec to fit the code and makes terminate-in-`starting` local while the launch races it).
WATCHOUT: a 409 refusal of the `starting` write after a successful acquire must NOT release the lease. `leaseStore.Acquire` is idempotent per holder, so two concurrent /starts on one replica both acquire, and releasing on the loser's 409 drops the winner's lease. Not releasing is safe: after the primary-read `ready` check, a refused row is either `starting`/later under this replica's concurrent start or terminal, and the Sweeper never renews a terminal row's lease. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:34-44; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:430-437 (terminal rows return before Acquire)
FACT: no sessionserver terminal path releases the coordination lease. `afterFailed` and `rollbackBinding` release the claim, slot, and binding only, and a terminal row's lease lapses on its TTL. The only explicit releases are the early-commit rollbacks. So TEST-1 item 9's "no ... lease held" assertion needs CODE-6 item 3's failure exits to call `releaseCoordinationLease`. That was already false for the post-`registerBinding` SetupOutput exit before this round. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_terminal.go:94; bind_rollback.go:27-51; create_and_start.go:510; delegated_child_materialize.go:198
FACT: the Sweeper renews any lease whose prior holder is this replica, in any non-terminal state except a podless `suspended` row, so a lease acquired at /start self-renews through a 600 s launch with no binding published. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:487-493

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 1 entry write sits after `InputWaits.Register` and before the `elicitation_request` publish; any non-`ErrSuperseded` error from it calls `InputWaits.Cancel(sessionID, requestID)` and returns the error, publishing nothing — BECAUSE the registry has no TTL (only Resolve/Cancel remove an entry), so an uncancelled registration pins `PendingForSession` non-empty and blocks item 2's exit write and item 3's stale-wait revert for that session on this replica; waiting on a `running` row lets the idle sweep act on a waiting session (F-11.3.39 again) — ALTERNATIVES: retry the write (rejected: item 2's no-retry RecordPodRelease precedent); keep waiting and add a sweeper repair `running → input_required` when a wait is pending (rejected: second mechanism mirroring item 3, hair); write before Register (rejected: item 3 can revert the row in the gap before Register, and a Register ErrDuplicate after an applied write strands the row until a pass).
FACT: inputwait.Registry.Register bumps the §8.8 one_shot `consumed` counter and Cancel never decrements it, so a cancelled entry-write failure spends a one_shot runtime's single round, exactly as a timeout or peer cancel does today — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/inputwait/inputwait.go:114-124, :170-178; mcptools_register.go:1015 (Consumed check), :1024 (Register).
WATCHOUT: `elicitation_request` is published between Register and the wait select today; the entry write must be placed before that publish, otherwise a failed write leaves the client holding a question whose wait was cancelled — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:1024-1052.

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
MISTAKE: The previous run's Edit (l) second insertion (path 3 "buffered messages stay in the inbox until the session leaves `input_required` ...") contradicted three existing §7.2 rules: the in-memory Crash recovery row (adopted inbox is empty, spec/07:305), durable-inbox takeover delivery (spec/07:316, :318), and **Delivery of held messages.** delivering path-3 entries on the next `ready_for_input` (spec/07:351). It also made spec/15:1604 "once the `request_input` resolves" contradict Edit (l). Deleted in spec round 1.
WATCHOUT: Do not stage any sentence stating when a stale `input_required` row's messages are delivered, in spec-changes, summary, or non-spec. Path 3, **Delivery of held messages.**, and the session-inbox Crash recovery rows own that outcome, and a pointer clause is redundant because Edit (l)'s first insertion already points at path 3. Carving the stale row out of those rules (hold-until-exit) was rejected as exception clauses on settled rules. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:305, :316, :318, :342, :351
DECISION: Edit (l)'s second half is now a §6.2 idle-clock `input_required` row replacement of "or the `lenny/request_input` call expires." with "or it leaves `input_required` ([§7.2](...#72-interactive-session-model))." — BECAUSE that is the only spec clause naming an exit a stale row cannot take; the locator is unique in spec/ — ALTERNATIVES: citing "§7.2 path 3" (path 3 is a routing rule and lists no exits); rewriting the whole row (the "not idle" reason holds for every live wait).
DECISION: No §15.4 edit and no qualifier at spec/06:190, spec/06:256, or spec/11:252 — BECAUSE once the hold-until-exit text is gone, spec/15:1604 matches path 3 (a stale row's call did resolve), and the others are definitional copies of the §7.2 sentence Edit (l) qualifies once beside it — ALTERNATIVES: qualifying each copy (restates the exception at every site).
FACT: "request_input` call expires" occurs in spec/ only at spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:279; docs/client-guide/session-lifecycle.md:81 mirrors it (separate finding for the non-spec loop, not staged here). — EVIDENCE: grep -rn over spec/ and docs/

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Edit (l) deletes its second (path 3) insertion outright and keeps only the §7.2 sub-state sentence that a session can stay `input_required` after its call ended; its §6.2 half replaces the idle-clock `input_required` row's "or the `lenny/request_input` call expires." with "or it leaves `input_required` ([§7.2](...))" — BECAUSE the existing §7.2 rules already give the stale row's message outcome (path 3 buffers on row state; **Delivery of held messages.** delivers path-3 entries on the next `ready_for_input`; the two Crash recovery rows cover replica exit), so any stale-row delivery sentence either restates them or contradicts them — ALTERNATIVES: a pointer clause in path 3 (rejected: the first insertion already points at path 3, one site is enough); keep hold-until-exit and carve it out of spec/07 Crash recovery rows and the held-delivery bullet (rejected: exception clauses, and it claims an in-memory inbox survives a replica exit); edit the §15.4 `immediate` row (rejected: once "no outstanding request remains to resolve" is deleted, it matches path 3 word for word and the stale row's call did resolve).
DECISION: the definitional copies (spec/06 `input_required` sub-state sentence, spec/06 maxSessionAge-table `input_required` row, docs state-machines.md) stay unedited — BECAUSE the §7.2 sub-state paragraph keeps the same "blocked inside" sentence and Edit (l) states the exception once beside it — ALTERNATIVES: qualify every copy (restatement, hair).
FACT: the tree has no `ready_for_input` delivery trigger. Held messages drain only before a path-2 delivery, and both routers send every message for an `input_required` row (or one with a pending wait) to path 3. So in the tree a stale row's inbox drains only once the row leaves `input_required`, which is why the summary Defects heading "holds the session's messages in the inbox" stays true of the tree even though the spec rule delivers on `ready_for_input` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/messagerouting/messagerouting.go:162-163, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/send_delivery.go:137-140, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:186-190
WATCHOUT: `$P/*spec-changes.md` also matches `non-spec-changes.md`, so sed/awk over that glob prints the concatenation and line numbers go wrong. Use the full file name — EVIDENCE: the proposal directory listing
WATCHOUT: do not add a TEST-1 case that asserts a stale row's inbox is delivered on `ready_for_input`. The tree has no such trigger, so the case fails until the filed defect lands — EVIDENCE: messagerouting.go:39-49

### [spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The CODE-7 item 3 row 10 adapter-reconnect case now has a second sentence in SPEC-2 Edit (l)'s §7.2 insertion: the session is `running` while a later call waits, so it is evaluated for idleness and its messages are routed as for a `running` session. The spec-changes Edge-cases bullet was widened in place to "rows 8 to 10", and D13's first clause now names row 10 as the one case in which an idle sweep lists a waiting row — BECAUSE the outcome is visible to clients and was stated only in proposal reasoning — ALTERNATIVES: skip the exit write when the request context ends (rejected: turns a reconnect into the stale row of rows 8 and 9 when no later wait arrives, and redesigns CODE-7); have the exit mutator consult a cross-replica wait record (rejected: that record is R20's store, which D13 declines); the reviewer's "idle evaluation only" clause (rejected: routing is part of the visible change).
WATCHOUT: Do not write "path 2" into the reconnect sentence. A `running` session with a `lenny/request_input` call in flight cannot be on path 2, because the adapter emits no `ready_for_input` while any tool call is in flight. Its messages go to path 5, or to path 4 for `delivery: "immediate"`. "Routed as for a `running` session" covers this without restating the path rules. The finding's path-2 claim was wrong — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:337-341 (`ready_for_input` normative definition, paths 4 and 5)
FACT: The definitional `input_required` copies (spec/06:256 and :279, spec/07:224, docs session-lifecycle.md:79/:81, concepts.md:77, state-machines.md:96, runtime-author-guide lifecycle.md:204, spec/15:1604) describe the `input_required` state and remain true. The reconnect session is `running`, so none of them needs an edit (this is consistent with the standing decision that leaves definitional copies unedited) — EVIDENCE: related lookup, spec.3.fix-G1

The spec-changes Edge-cases stale/reconnect bullet is a pending-redesign note. non-spec CODE-7 carries a pending-redesign note for items 1-3, content unchanged. ### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: Close the input_required interrupt/terminate gap with a new SPEC-2 Edit (l) that legalises what the code already admits (pkg/api/v1/session/session.go preconditionTable EndpointInterrupt {Running, InputRequired}, EndpointTerminate incl. InputRequired). Edit (l) adds `input_required → suspended` and `input_required → completed` lines to the §7.2 block and the §6.2 `input_required` block, adds `input_required` to the §15.1 interrupt and terminate rows, adds it to the §29.3 off-holder interrupt and terminate rows, and cuts the two §29.4 restatements of the interrupt precondition to pointers. BECAUSE today a waiting row reads `running`, so interrupt and terminate already work on a waiting session; CODE-7 makes the row read `input_required`, and the applied spec would then contradict reachable code. ALTERNATIVES: code refuses interrupt in input_required (breaks 0101 CODE-2 forwardStates and its tier test item 8 "the input_required case discriminates", and regresses clients); Defects-only bullet (leaves spec contradicting reachable code that 0105 makes reachable); widening the §7.2 "all failure transitions ... also apply" sentence (its "(the state machine above lists them)" turns false and §6.2 stays incomplete); a general "every precondition for running applies to input_required" rule (flips the settled upload/stale-derive Keep in CODE-7 item 4).

WATCHOUT: Upload and stale-derive differ from interrupt/terminate: there the code refuses input_required and agrees with §15.1, so they stay in summary Defects. Do not "harmonise" them into Edit (l). ### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed no spec finding — BECAUSE every staged spec edit either rides an existing state-change write (SPEC-1 state_entered_at and the Edit (b) last_agent_activity_at stamp on entry to running) or codifies shipped replica placement (SPEC-4); no new etcd/CRD write, Redis key, or watch — ALTERNATIVES: filing SPEC-4's every-replica sweep listing as a Tier 3 bottleneck was rejected because it is shipped behavior (watchdog DefaultTickInterval 5 s on every replica, pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:180) tracked as F-10.1.36 and listed in summary Non-goals/Defects.
FACT: Tier 3 sizing used for the math: 10,000 active sessions, 5-30 gateway replicas, 400 pods per replica — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:7, :986, :1119.
FACT: the ready → starting write before launch does not worsen coordinator loss: a /start replica crash leaves a `starting` row the 120 s maxStartingTimeoutSeconds watchdog fails, where the shipped `ready` row waited on the ready watchdog — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:257.
DEFERRED [0105_fix_the-per-state-session-deadlines-and-the-watchdog-sweeps.non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-7 W4 (line 159) re-arms every connection record on the replica on any failed W1/W2, and R1 (line 160) updates every session GetActiveSlotsByPod lists, with A3 (line 180) rerunning R1 on every next call while reads fail. During and after a Postgres failover this is up to 30 replicas x 400 connections of R1 runs (GetByID + GetActiveSlotsByPod + a locked update per listed slot) in a burst, and the staging does not say whether a failed R1 blocks or still dispatches the call. The non-spec loop should check the burst size and the dispatch-on-R1-failure behavior. This is a code-only concern; no spec edit closes it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: W4 fires only on a failed W2 — BECAUSE W1's other-error branch always calls Cancel and takes W2, so a committed W1 reaches X1 or X4 (which re-arms) and an uncommitted one reaches X3; row E4 stays as written — ALTERNATIVES: adding the re-arm to E4 (keeps a redundant, untested leg).
DECISION: one replica-wide re-arm generation per GatewayControl server, referenced by every connection record via NewContext; a record is recovered while its recovered generation equals the replica's — BECAUSE it re-arms every connection without a record set — ALTERNATIVES: none recorded here, see WATCHOUTs.
WATCHOUT: do not add a replica-wide set of live connection records (TagConn insert, ConnEnd removal, walk in W4). It was the reviewer's suggestion; it leaks a record per connection if removal is missed, walks under a lock on every W4, and needs a removal test. The shared generation needs none of it. Also do not make the generation package-level: TEST-1 item 8's K1 case builds a second server in one process.
WATCHOUT: R1 scopes by the connection's verified agent SPIFFE identity (PodName) compared with the GetByID row's PodAssignment; the request session_id is the pod's own claim — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/auth.go:27-33 ("resolves the caller's tenant from the session_id in the request body and has no other proof of identity"); cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:156-160; pkg/mtls/spiffe/spiffe.go:8,55,69 (agent URI, Identity.PodName, Parse). A mismatched run writes nothing and leaves the record not recovered, so the pod's next own-session call runs R1. Do not add a plaintext-dev fallback to the row's pod (second mode; reopens the moved-session case); on that path R1 never runs and row P1 covers the stale row.
FACT: the only stats handler on GatewayControl is otelgrpc (gwcontrol_server.go:180); the interceptor chain is RequireVerifiedPeerInterceptor then RequireSATokenInterceptor (:172-175); RequireVerifiedPeerInterceptor passes everything through when clientCA is empty.
FACT: edge-cases bullet "A stale input_required row ..." (non-spec-changes Edge cases) described P1's old Case cell; replaced in place with "that no later call of its pod process recovers" so it covers P1's widened case. The design had not adjudicated that site.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: W4 is triggered by a failed W2 only ("A W2 write that fails"); the W1 leg is deleted — BECAUSE W1's other-error branch always calls Cancel and takes W2, so a committed W1 reaches X1 or X4 (which re-arms) and an uncommitted one reaches X3 with nothing stale; row E4 is then consistent with the rules — ALTERNATIVES: adding the re-arm to row E4 (keeps a redundant trigger and a second untested leg).
DECISION: W4 re-arms through ONE replica-wide re-arm generation that every connection record references (pointer handed in at NewContext/TagConn); a record is recovered while the generation it last recorded equals the replica's; R1 reads the replica generation at run start and, on a complete run, raises the record's recovered generation to that value — BECAUSE it needs no replica-wide record set, no insertion at TagConn, no removal at *stats.ConnEnd, no lock walk, and cannot leak; the "generation unchanged since the run started" check falls out — ALTERNATIVES: a replica-wide set with TagConn insert and ConnEnd removal (the reviewer's suggestion: adds a per-connection lifecycle, a leak hazard, and a removal test); re-arming only the record on the failing call's context (misses row A2, where the wait sits on the pod's earlier connection); a package-level counter (two servers in one test process would share it).
DECISION: R1 takes its pod scope from the connection's verified agent SPIFFE identity: R1 runs only on a connection whose peer presents one, and after GetByID continues only when the row's PodAssignment equals the identity's pod name; a run that stops on a mismatch writes and records nothing (the record stays not recovered) — BECAUSE GatewayControl handlers trust the body session_id (cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:156-160, leasecontrol/auth.go:27-33) and the SPIFFE URI is the only authenticated pod identity — ALTERNATIVES: listing GetActiveSlotsByPod(peer pod) and dropping the session read (still needs a row for the Update's TenantID, so no saving); falling back to the session row's pod on the plaintext path (a second mode, and it reopens the benign moved-session case); marking the record recovered after a mismatch (a pod's later calls for its own sessions would never run R1).
DECISION: on the local-development plaintext GatewayControl path (no clientCA, gwcontrol_server.go:44-48) R1 never runs; a stale row there falls to row P1 (client terminate or maxSessionAge). D13 and rows X4/K1 are left as written because R1 is the home of its trigger condition; only P1's Case cell changes to "no later call of the pod process runs R1".
FACT: PodAssignment is the Sandbox name (sessionserver/create.go:363, bind_registration.go:129); §10.3 resolves the SPIFFE {pod-name} from the SandboxClaim/Sandbox records (spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.3 "Gateway validates pod identity on every inbound handshake"). EVIDENCE: pkg/mtls/spiffe/spiffe.go Identity.PodName, Parse.
WATCHOUT: no helper today extracts the peer SPIFFE identity on GatewayControl; leasecontrol/auth.go peerHasVerifiedCert only checks VerifiedChains. R1 parses the URI SAN of the verified leaf (VerifiedChains[0][0]); with trustDomain empty (CA-only) the handshake did not validate the URI, so R1 must still require Kind agent. EVIDENCE: gwcontrol_server.go:132-147.
WATCHOUT: a fresh record must start below the replica generation so the first session-ID call runs R1; TEST-1 item 8's fixture must be mTLS with client certs carrying the agent SPIFFE URI of the rows' pod, or every R1 case passes vacuously. EVIDENCE: gwcontrol_server.go:46-49 plaintext path.

FACT: sessionserver Server.recordSessionCompleted wraps ctx in terminalContext = context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), budget); the watchdog's no-terminal fallback (archiveChild, billing Append) and orphan cleanup's archiveOrphan use ctx directly — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:2000-2006, usage.go:331-338, watchdog.go:1587-1600
FACT: orphan cleanup's expireOrphan opens a claim only when s.terminal is non-nil (end is a no-op otherwise), so the leak is on the OnSessionTerminal branch; archiveOrphan has no claim — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/orphancleanup/orphancleanup.go:235-239, :264-272

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: GatewayControl handlers trust the session_id in the request body; the only caller proof is the mTLS chain plus the NET-060 SPIFFE URI `agent/{pool}/{pod}` checked at handshake and the SA token. Nothing binds the named session to the calling pod. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:159-160, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/auth.go:27-32, pkg/mtls/spiffe/spiffe.go:8
WATCHOUT: CODE-7 R1 takes its pod scope from the row of the session the call names (GetByID, then GetActiveSlotsByPod on that row's pod), not from the connection's verified peer pod. A call that names a session whose row names another pod (an untrusted runtime, or an old pod that reconnects after its session resumed onto a new pod) makes R1 restore that other pod's live waits to `running`. D13's premise that the connection belongs to the pod the row names is unchecked. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:160; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:381 (the deny list covers only security terminations, so a replaced pod can reconnect)
DECISION: filed the R1 trust-boundary finding with a non-spec fix (compare the row's PodAssignment with the peer certificate's pod and skip R1 on a mismatch or when no peer identity exists, plus a TEST-1 item 8 case) — BECAUSE the fix sits in CODE-7 and needs no spec edit — ALTERNATIVES: binding every GatewayControl call's session to the peer, which is wider than this change and is pre-existing exposure, rejected
FACT: The running-only gates outside CODE-7 item 4 that matter for security (orphanEligibleStates, idleClockRunningStates, classifyIdleWrite) already handle input_required, or omit it correctly. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/orphansession/orphansession.go:68-74, pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1160-1162

### [non-spec.4.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: Decline the expiry-warning half of F-11.3.40. Add one summary Defects bullet, which REC-1 item 5 files, and narrow REC-1 item 1's F-11.3.40 note to the sweep-write half with a pointer to item 5. Do not stage a re-check in `sweepExpiryWarning` — BECAUSE the caller directive settles CODE-2. SPEC-4's locked-row rule governs a sweep's *write*, and the warning writes nothing, so a `store.Get` re-read only narrows the window and does not close it. The remedy that closes it, a persisted warned stamp written under the row lock, also closes the cross-replica duplicate bullet, so the two defects should be fixed together — ALTERNATIVES: a Get re-read before `OnSessionExpiringSoon` (rejected: it touches settled CODE-2, adds a partial mechanism, and needs its own TEST-1 case); folding the new defect into the existing "**Duplicate `session_expiring_soon` emissions across replicas.**" bullet (rejected: different defect, and REC-1 item 5 files one entry per bullet).
FACT: `sweepExpiryWarning` lists every row per tenant and calls `OnSessionExpiringSoon` serially with no re-read. Each call can block for up to `interruptDeadline` on `SignalDeadline`, so the listing that later rows act on can be stale by several seconds rather than microseconds — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1425-1457; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/expiry_warning.go (signalDeadlineApproaching, context.WithTimeout(ctx, s.interruptDeadline())).
FACT: The BUILD-GAPS F-11.3.40 Gap line names both halves: "...expire an incarnation that re-entered or became active after the listing, and emit session_expiring_soon after a terminal write." The proposal's problem statement (lines 15 and 83) validated only the sweep-write half — EVIDENCE: BUILD-GAPS.md:18800.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-4's §10.1.1 sentence ("A watchdog sweep's write re-evaluates the sweep's deadline on the locked session row") does not reach the expiry warning, so declining leaves no code-versus-staged-spec divergence. Do not file the warning as a SPEC-4 violation — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-4 staged paragraph.

### [non-spec.4.review-performance.1]

- FACT: R1's first read, pgstore `GetByID`, routes to the read replica (`s.read`, InAllTenants), while `GetActiveSlotsByPod` reads the primary (`s.pool`, InAllTenants). Replica lag makes the pod-identity stop take A3 and retry on the next call; it never mis-writes, because the locked-row mutator decides state. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:527-545, :1063-1077
- FACT: pgstore `Update` takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` per call, so each R1 listed session costs one locked transaction even when the mutator returns ErrSuperseded. At Tier 3 (10,000 sessions, 400 per replica, ~1,300/s sustained against a ~1,600/s ceiling, spec/12 §12.3) a replica restart costs about 400 R1 runs, each one replica read, one primary read, and K locked txns, staggered by reconnect. That is below the burst headroom and is not a finding. EVIDENCE: pgstore.go:557, :624
- FACT: the `gateway-control` shutdown step calls `GracefulStop` with no deadline, before the `postgres` step, so C1's claim that every handler's W2 (5 s bound, DefaultPrimaryReadTimeout) finishes before the pool closes holds. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:142-152; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/primary_read.go:16
- WATCHOUT: the message router classifies a persisted `input_required` row as path 3 (inbox buffer) whatever InputWaits holds. A stale row after X4 or K1 that no later call recovers (row P1) therefore buffers client messages until the runtime's next ready_for_input, which an idle runtime never emits. This is the cost of the owner's D13 rejection of a retrier or a time-bounded restore. Do not refile it as a finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:188-190; pkg/gateway/session/messagerouting/messagerouting.go:162
- FACT: migration 0192 `ADD COLUMN ... NOT NULL DEFAULT now()` takes the fast-default path (now() is STABLE), so the migration does not rewrite the table.

### [non-spec.4.review-security.1]
FACT: The GatewayControl platform-tool bridge already dispatches under whatever session ID the adapter presents, with no pod-to-session binding check; the adapter is treated as trusted mesh-authenticated infrastructure. So CODE-7 W1/W2 writing the call's session row without a pod check is the shipped trust model, not a regression. R1's PodAssignment-equals-SPIFFE-pod check is stricter than the bridge. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:156-160 ("the handlers trust the session_id in the request body"); pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/platformtools.go:97-99; pkg/gateway/gatewaycontrol/platformtools/platformtools.go:20-26
FACT: spiffe.Parse returns Kind agent or interceptor and does not check the trust domain; the handshake checks the trust domain only when clientCA and trustDomain are both set. TEST-1 item 24 (tier 9) therefore runs with trustDomain empty so that the interceptor-URI case reaches R1. EVIDENCE: pkg/mtls/spiffe/spiffe.go:69-125; cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:128-146
FACT: Tenant pinning holds on every recycle pool, including allowCrossTenantReuse, so the cross-tenant GetActiveSlotsByPod listing that R1 updates under the call's TenantID cannot name rows from another tenant. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:459, :469; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1086-1088
DECISION: filed no security findings in non-spec round 4 — BECAUSE no established control is removed and no security bound is sourced from a pod self-report or reset on a store outage — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the absence of a pod check on W1 as a cross-session write (rejected, because it is the pre-existing bridge trust model)

### [non-spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the CODE-6 item 1 `/start` exits row S1 Coordination lease cell reads "as step (c) leaves it, and renewed by the Sweeper" — BECAUSE step (c) is the single home of the lease-acquire predicate (acquire only when the entry row's `PodAssignment` is non-empty), and the old cell "held, and renewed by the Sweeper" contradicted it and TEST-1 item 9's service-mode sub-case — ALTERNATIVES: copying the PodAssignment/service-mode/no-binder predicate into the cell (a third statement), deleting TEST-1 item 9's service-mode sub-case (it is the discriminating assertion for step (c)).
WATCHOUT: the F-row and X-row "released" lease cells stay true for a podless row because release of an unacquired lease is a no-op; do not qualify them with step (c)'s predicate — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 1 `/start` exits table, rows F1-X2.

### [non-spec.6.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: A W2 that commits and then errors is its own CODE-7 item 3 row, X5 (row as X1 or X2 leaves it, nothing published, W4 re-arms, recovery as X1 or X2); X4 narrows to "W2 fails before the store commits" — BECAUSE the committed case is a different outcome (row already right, only the status_change is lost), and a separate row keeps X4's row cell, its R1 (A1 or A4) recovery cell, and P1's "After K1 or X4" true with no conditionals — ALTERNATIVES: an "or committed" clause in X4 (would falsify X4's recovery cell and P1); publishing status_change(running) after a W2 error or from R1 on a `running` row (neither can tell whether the write committed); deleting the completeness claim (leaves the path untested).
WATCHOUT: Do not add a re-read or publish-after-error step to W2 or R1 to recover X5's lost status_change(running). It is a lost acknowledgement under a double failure, the same as E4's committed W1, and W3 already says a failed write publishes nothing — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:753 (Update reports success when writeApplied reads back last_write_id), :769 writeApplied.
DECISION: E4's recovery-owner cell reads "This wait's W2" with no outcome list, matching E1 and E2 — BECAUSE an enumeration of W2 outcomes omitted X2 once and goes stale with each new X row — ALTERNATIVES: "(X1 to X4)" or "(X1 to X5)", rejected for the same reason.
OPEN: Row A2's recovery cell "That wait's W2 (X1 or X2)" has the same enumeration defect (it omits X3 to X5). It predates this round and was left for a later round by the G2 design; the fix is the same deletion to "That wait's W2".

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: /start exits row S1's Coordination lease cell becomes "as step (c) leaves it, and renewed by the Sweeper", a pointer to step (c), which is the one home of the lease predicate — BECAUSE the cell restated the outcome without the `PodAssignment` predicate and contradicted TEST-1 item 9's service-mode sub-case — ALTERNATIVES: copying the `PodAssignment` predicate into the cell (a third statement of the rule); deleting the TEST-1 item 9 service-mode sub-case (it is the assertion that discriminates step (c)'s predicate).
WATCHOUT: review-log entries at review-log.md:254 and :273 record an earlier acquire-before-write order. CODE-6 item 1 step (c) now places the acquire after the step (b) write. Do not "restore" acquire-first from those entries — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 1, sentence "The write precedes the acquire so that the locked row alone serializes concurrent starts".

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: A W2 that commits and then reports an error gets its own row, X5 ("As X1 or X2 leaves it | Nothing | As X1 or X2. W4 re-arms ... | As X1 or X2"), and X4's Case narrows to "W2 fails before the store commits". BECAUSE folding the committed case into X4 (as E4 does) would make X4's row cell, X4's recovery-owner cell (R1 via A1 or A4), and P1's "After K1 or X4" each conditional; a separate row leaves all three true and unedited. ALTERNATIVES: an "or" clause in X4 (reviewer's suggestion; this breaks P1 and the X4 recovery cell); publishing status_change(running) after a W2 error, or having R1 publish when it finds `running` (neither can tell whether the write committed, and the uncommitted case would emit a false or duplicate event).
DECISION: The status_change(running) a committed-then-errored W2 loses is accepted as a lost acknowledgement, the same outcome E4's committed W1 already has for status_change(input_required). The rules (W3 and W4) already fix the behaviour, so no rule changes and no staged text defends it. BECAUSE pgstore.Update reads back last_write_id on a commit error, so this outcome needs both the commit ack and the read-back to fail. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:549-554, :760-778 (writeApplied).
DECISION: E4's recovery-owner cell becomes "This wait's W2", the wording E1 and E2 already use, and the parenthetical list is deleted. BECAUSE round 1 kept the list and round 6 found it incomplete (it lacked X2). Extending it to "X1 to X5" repeats the same answer and goes stale on the next row added.
WATCHOUT: Row A2's recovery-owner cell "That wait's W2 (X1 or X2)" has the same defect as E4 already had. That wait's W2 can also take X3, X4, or X5. This is pre-existing, so it is left for a later round. The fix there is the same deletion, to "That wait's W2". EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 item 3, row A2.
WATCHOUT: X4's row cell ("`input_required`, naming a wait no registry holds") is already imprecise for a W2 that fails before it reads the row, for example after E3 or on an N2 row. That row is left unchanged in whatever state it had, and the earlier row's owner still holds it. This is a separate finding and this round did not stage it.
FACT: The summary's "emits `status_change` on both edges" and the REC-1 F-11.3.39 note were already non-universal under E4's committed W1 (W3 publishes nothing on a failed write). X5 only puts into the table an outcome the rules already produced. Do not qualify those sites as part of this fix.

### [non-spec.6.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: X5 keeps "Nothing" published; REC-1 item 3 and summary line 12 are qualified instead (REC-1: "under CODE-7 item 2's W3"; summary: "on each edge its write reports applied") — BECAUSE the handler cannot tell a committed-then-errored write from an uncommitted one (pgstore Update already resolves a lost commit ack by write-ID read-back where it can, so X5 is the residual double failure), so "publish after a committed error" is not implementable — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's option 1 (W3 publishes on X5) rejected for that reason and per non-spec.6.fix-G2's WATCHOUT against publish-after-error.
FACT: pgstore Update stamps a per-call last_write_id and reads it back after a lost commit acknowledgement (§12.3) — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:615-620
DECISION: Row A2's recovery-owner cell reduced to "That wait's W2", matching E1, E2, and E4 — closes the OPEN in non-spec.6.fix-G2.
WATCHOUT: Do not re-add "as §7.2 requires" or "on both edges" for status_change in REC-1 or the summary; E4's committed W1 and X5 publish nothing by W3.

### [non-spec.6.review-reliability.1]
FACT: Commit 0eeed1c4e (D13 owner decision) deleted the summary Defects bullet "A stale `input_required` row holds the session's messages in the inbox". The defect behind it outlives the stale-row concept: no gateway code drains the inbox on `input_required → running`. deliverBuffered (held_delivery.go:52) runs only from deliverNow, resume, commitStart, the resume tail and takeover delivery (resume_driver.go:774 skips `input_required` rows), and DeliverHeld. Both routers send every message for an `input_required` row to path 3 (messagerouting.go:162, messages_delivery.go:188, send_delivery.go:138). Once CODE-7 persists the state, a non-reply message sent during any live wait, on any replica, stays in the inbox after W2 (X1) or R1 (A1). Filed as a missing summary Defects entry. — EVIDENCE: git show 0eeed1c4e summary.md; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/held_delivery.go:52
FACT: The coordination Sweeper renews a lease when the session is bound here or the lease is already self-held, even if the session is unbound (coordination.go:490). PodHeld/adoptedState covers running, input_required, and suspended with a pod, and excludes starting. So a crashed /start leaves a `starting` row that nobody adopts, and the starting watchdog fails it. In the F5 committed-write case the replica's own Sweeper re-acquires the lease it released, or a peer adopts it with a fence. Neither leaks anything. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:316, :490-520
FACT: The lease store wraps Redis with a Postgres advisory-lock Failover (leasestore/failover.go), so CODE-6 row F1 "another lease-store error" fires only when Redis and Postgres both fail. Do not file F1 against the §12.4 lease-fallback row. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:13-75
FACT: Every GatewayControl RPC is unary (schemas/lenny-adapter.proto), so a unary R1 interceptor sees every platform tool call. The server sets no keepalive or MaxConnectionAge options. request_input always has a bounded timeout (defaultRequestInputTimeout, mcptools_register.go:937-939), so a half-open old stream releases its wait. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:154-181
DECISION: Did not refile "F1 permanently fails a session on a transient lease-store error". BECAUSE the Failover exists, and D8 (settled) puts every post-write failure on `starting → failed`. ALTERNATIVES: none worth keeping.

### [non-spec.6.review-security.1]
FACT: The R1 pod check (row `PodAssignment` equals the SPIFFE pod segment) works because the Sandbox controller names the backing Pod after the Sandbox, so `PodName == SandboxName` — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/controller.go:975 ("want.PodName = live.Name").
FACT: The platform tool bridge trusts the adapter-supplied session_id with no pod-to-session check (this predates the proposal). W1 inherits that trust, and only R1 adds a pod check. The bridge already lets a compromised adapter act under any session, so W1 does not regress an established control — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/gatewaycontrol/platformtools/platformtools.go:105-122; cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:163-175.
FACT: Every pod stays pinned to one tenant for its whole lifetime, `allowCrossTenantReuse` included, so R1 updating the `GetActiveSlotsByPod` rows under the call session's TenantID never meets a row from another tenant — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:456 ("The pin persists ... including a pool with microvm-gated `recycle.allowCrossTenantReuse`").
DECISION: Did not file the case where `starting` joins `isPreRunningClaimState`, so a replica without the binding can by-name release a slot while another replica is mid-bind — BECAUSE a by-name claim DELETE always retires the pod (spec/04 §4.7.10 "retires the pod when it ends the occupancy without reuse"; podsession/reclaim.go:79-84). The per-slot path can briefly under-count occupancy, but only for sessions of the same tenant, and a later leaked release re-adds `leaked:<id>` (slotcounter.go:262-275). That is capacity, not a security bound, and CODE-6 item 4 is settled — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a residual-state regression, rejected for lack of any cross-tenant or reuse path.
WATCHOUT: usage.go:459-466 has a doc comment that explains why `starting` was excluded from `isPreRunningClaimState`. CODE-6 item 4 does not stage a rewrite of that comment, so the implementor has to update it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:462-465.

### [orchestrator.prune-code7]
DECISION: The run stopped with verdict `prune`. CODE-7 item 3's outcome table claimed to list every outcome of W0 to W4, R1, and C1, and TEST-1 items 7 and 8 mirrored it row by row, so each round found another missing combination or an owner cell that contradicted a rule. The rules and D13 were stable, so the table is cut back rather than extended.
DECISION: CODE-7 item 3 now states that W0 to W4, R1, and C1 are normative and that the table lists only the cases they leave implicit. Kept rows: E0, E4, X4, X5, K1, N1, N2, A2, A3, and P1. Deleted rows: E1, E2, E3, X1, X2, X3, A1, and A4, each an applied or superseded branch of W1, W2, or R1. The Published and `InputWaits` columns are dropped; the facts they carried for kept rows are either stated by W1, W3, and W4 or folded into the Case cell. Kept rows that referred to deleted rows (X4, X5, N1, and N2) now refer to the rules.
DECISION: TEST-1 item 7 tests W0 to W3 directly, with their applied and superseded branches, plus rows E0 and E4. TEST-1 item 8 tests W4, R1, and C1 directly, plus rows X4, X5, K1, A2, A3, and P1, and adds a seeded randomized interleaving of wait entry, exit, W2 failure, and connection accept with an invariant checker that no row is `input_required` without a live wait once recovery has run. Item 17 keeps N1 and N2. Item 14 now names W1 and W2 instead of rows E1 and X1.
FACT: No other file referenced a deleted CODE-7 row ID. The X1, X2, and P1 IDs that remain in the summary and non-spec-changes belong to CODE-6 item 1's `/start` exits table, which this prune leaves untouched.
DECISION: Standing context compacted: D13 Settled entry rewritten to the staged mechanism; OPEN and DEFERRED on an OpenAPI `STARTING_FAILED` description closed (`pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json` carries no `STARTING_FAILED`); CODE-4 item 2 orphan-cleanup OPEN closed (item 2 names `OnSessionTerminal` and `archiveOrphan`); OpenAPI state-enum UNVERIFIED closed (openapi.json lists `input_required`); a prune Trap added.

OPEN: After an interrupt from `input_required` (Edit (l)) and a later `suspended → running` resume, the `lenny/request_input` wait can still be pending: no code path cancels InputWaits on an interrupt (only mcptools_register.go:1074,1119 call Cancel). The row then reads `running` while a wait is live, so idle evaluation counts the session as idle until maxRequestInputWaitSeconds ends the wait. This narrows D13's "idle evaluation skips a waiting session". It was not filed because the spec does not say what an interrupt does to a pending tool call. A non-spec or owner round should decide whether it belongs in the summary's Defects list.

ALTERNATIVES: (1) /start terminal-on-failure as a Postgres-failover regression against shipped `ready` retry. Rejected: D8 and the Non-goals settle it, and the directive bars reopening D1 to D14. FACT: Orphan cleanup is built unguarded on every replica in cmd/lenny-gateway/reconciler_workers.go:46 and appears in no leaderGate.Add in leader_sweeps.go, so SPEC-4's "every replica ... orphan cleanup" matches the tree. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:161-477
FACT: Proxy-mode activity is held in memory and flushed to Postgres at most once per second (spec/06:271), so the SPEC-4 locked-row idle recheck can miss up to 1 s of activity. The adapter seal check in §6.2 idle suspension (spec/06:289) bounds it. Not a finding. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:271, :289

WATCHOUT (not filed, below the bar): a request_input whose W1 finds a `suspended` row returns ErrSuperseded, and the wait proceeds. This can happen after an INTERRUPT_TIMEOUT, if the runtime keeps running. A later suspended → running then leaves a live, unpersisted wait. §28.5.3 assumes that a runtime past the interrupt deadline has stopped making progress.
