FACT: A step (c) `/start` lease (60 s TTL) held across a launch longer than the TTL is renewed by the coordination Sweeper's `priorHolder == replicaID` branch, and `starting` is never adopted, so CODE-6's lease-before-launch order does not lapse. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:489-490, :335-342; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:34-45
FACT: `GetActiveSlotsByPod` returns IDs through the migration 0080/0191 indexes; R1 costs per pod one GetByID, one indexed listing, and one locked txn per slot (most superseded). A full-fleet reconnect at Tier 3 is ~30k ops spread over the rollout, comparable to adoption writes. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1085-1091
DECISION: no performance finding filed — BECAUSE every remaining cost (W1/W2 two writes per human-paced wait, trigger CPU on updateSQL, R1 per connection, leader-gated reconciler reducing per-pass work and apiserver fallback calls ~25x) stays within existing budgets — ALTERNATIVES: filing W4's replica-wide re-arm granularity (one failed W2 makes every connection on the replica, ~400 at Tier 3, run R1 on its next call; under a degraded Postgres this is a positive-feedback amplification) was rejected only because the caller directive marks W0-W4 and R1 settled.
OPEN: W4 re-arms the whole replica on any failed W2; narrowing it to connections whose peer pod is the failed wait's pod would remove the O(connections-per-replica) R1 burst during Postgres degradation. Owner question only if W4 is ever reopened.
DECISION: DOC-1 need not mirror `starting → failed` in docs/reference/state-machines.md — BECAUSE the page's `failed` row already says "other unrecoverable error", so the omission leaves it incomplete rather than false (Standing context "Docs omit starting → failed") — ALTERNATIVES: adding it is harmless polish, left to the docs lens.

### [non-spec.3.review-performance.1]
FACT: The coordination lease store is wrapped in a Redis-to-Postgres failover, so a Redis reset does not drive CODE-6 row F1 (a lease-store error at the step (c) acquire). F1 is reached only when both stores are down. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/leasestore/failover.go:13-16, :67-75; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:220
FACT: The shipped `lenny/request_input` handler already reads the row with `deps.Store.Get` before it registers, so the CODE-7 W1 Postgres dependency makes request_input no less available during a Postgres failover than it is today. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:984-990
FACT: The `gateway-control` shutdown step calls an unbounded `GracefulStop` before the `postgres` step, which matches CODE-7 C1's ordering claim. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:142-150
DECISION: Filed nothing under the performance lens — BECAUSE no step adds etcd/CRD writes or a net-new watch. CODE-7 costs two row writes per request_input, and R1 costs 2 reads plus one locked txn per slot, once per connection and once per W4 re-arm. CODE-6 costs one extra row write per /start. CODE-8 reduces apiserver and mirror load by gating the reconciler to the leader — ALTERNATIVES: filing the W4 replica-wide re-arm burst. Rejected because it is one locked txn per active session on the replica, spread over subsequent calls, and D13 settled the replica-wide generation.

### [non-spec.3.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed nothing under the reliability lens in non-spec round 3. BECAUSE every crash, restart, and store-failure path I traced ends in a stated owner: CODE-6 rows P3, X2, and F5 fall to the `starting` watchdog plus the by-name reclaim, or to the Sweeper renewing a bound session. CODE-7 rows X4, X5, K1, A2, and A3 fall to R1 or W2, and P1 is accepted under D13. ALTERNATIVES: filing R1's piggyback re-run with no backoff after A3 or W4 as a store stampede. Rejected because each connection runs R1 at most once per call and once per generation, and the capacity math belongs to the performance lens.
FACT: Shutdown runs stopListener, then waitTerminalWork, then sessions.Close(), and only then the closers (trace, llm-proxy, gateway-control, postgres). So C1's InputWaits.Close runs after the session server's lifetime is cancelled. W2 runs on WithoutCancel(request ctx) and is unaffected. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/shutdown.go:63-73, :142-153; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1895-1899
FACT: The GatewayControl server sets no server keepalive and no MaxConnectionAge. A half-open pod connection therefore ends a wait only on the handler's callTimeout (requestInputTimeout, or the runtime's MaxRequestInputWaitSeconds), which then runs W2. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:152-181; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:994-1004, :1073
FACT: Message routing already treats a row in `input_required` as path 3 (inbox buffer), keyed on the row state OR the local registry. Under CODE-7, a stale `input_required` row (P1) therefore buffers client messages until the runtime next enters ready_for_input, and does not deliver them right away. This is inside D13's acceptance of P1, so do not refile it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go:188-190; pkg/gateway/session/messagerouting/messagerouting.go:162
FACT: GetActiveSlotsByPod spans all tenants and includes terminal rows still inside the post-terminal window. R1's Update of those rows returns ErrSuperseded, because the mutator requires `input_required`. §5.2 tenant pinning keeps the call's TenantID correct for every listed row. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/memstore/memstore.go:447-465; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454
FACT: No CODE-7 row reference dangles. The kept rows E0, E4, X4, X5, K1, N1, N2, A2, A3, and P1 are the only ones cited (non-spec TEST-1 items 7, 8, and 17, item 1, W4, and Edge cases), and the summary cites only CODE-6 row X1. EVIDENCE: grep over summary, spec-changes, and checklist

### [non-spec.3.review-security.1]
- FACT: GatewayControl platform-tool dispatch trusts the request body's session_id with no check that the session's PodAssignment matches the caller's SPIFFE pod; any verified agent cert can dispatch on any session. Pre-existing, and wider than CODE-7 (delegate_task etc.); CODE-7 W1 inherits it, while R1 adds its own PodAssignment check. Not filed as a 0105 regression. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/gwcontrol_server.go:160-164 ("the handlers trust the session_id in the request body"), pkg/gateway/gatewaycontrol/platformtools/platformtools.go:105-123
- FACT: R1's "§5.2 Tenant pinning" basis for updating every GetActiveSlotsByPod row under the call's tenant holds on every pool, including allowCrossTenantReuse pools (the pin persists for the pod's lifetime). — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:454, :459, :469; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1074-1091
- FACT: ReclaimClaimed never returns a pod to reuse (exclusive: claim DELETE drains; per-slot: ReleaseSlotReservation never recycles), so CODE-6 item 4's `starting` by-name reclaim does not regress one-session-only reuse. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:64-109
- USEFUL [Traps "Not a security regression"]: confirmed; the by-name per-slot reclaim leak-accounting question stays a candidate unstaged defect rather than a finding.
- DECISION: security lens filed nothing in non-spec round 3 — BECAUSE the round-2 edits (DOC-1 starting→failed, TEST-1 item 22 tier-3 parity case, 0101 overlap row) touch no control, and the trusted sources (store-stamped state_entered_at, gateway idFn wait ID, verified SPIFFE plus PodAssignment check in R1, gateway-written child lease) hold — ALTERNATIVES: filing the W1 cross-session write was rejected as pre-existing and not a 0105 bypass.
